Ura-Sarugaku-cho, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 645](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n645/mode/1up))

**Miyairi, Keinosuke** M.D., Emeritus Prof. of Kyushu Imp. Univ.; b. '06; grad. from Med. Coll., Kyoto Imp. Univ.; Prof. at Med. Coll. Kyushu Imp. Univ., '06-27; is an authority on parasitology. Add. Fukuoka, Kyushu. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 645](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n645/mode/1up))

**Miyajima, Mikinosuke** M.D., Pathologist, M.P., Editor of Japan Medical World, Mem. of Kitazato Infectious Disease Institute; b. 1882 in Yamagata-ken; edu. at Science Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '98; further at the post-grad. course till '00; Lecturer on Zoology at the alma mater, '00; studied insects with special bearing to diseases at the post-grad. course, Kyoto Univ. in '01; was Lecturer at the Univ. the same year; expert at the Vaccine Laboratory, Tokyo, '03; sent to U.S.A., '04; expert at the Govt. Infectious Disease Institute, '05; sent to Philippines, '09; was granted the degree by submitting thesis, '09; removed to the present Institute in '14; was Japanese delegate at the 2nd International Health Conference at Geneva, '19; Dir. of Sanitary Dept. of the Tokyo Municipality, '21-23; visited U.S.A., '26, at the invitation of the Rockefeller Institute. Add. Kanda Jimbo-cho, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 645](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n645/mode/1up))

**Miyake, Hiidzu** M.D., Mem. House of Peers, Mem. Imperial Academy, Emeritus Professor of Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1848 in Tokyo; educated in France; was Prof. and Dean of Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. Add. Koishikawa Takehaya-cho, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 645](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n645/mode/1up))

**Nagayo, Mataro** M.D., Prof. of Pathology & Patho. Anatomy at Tokyo Imp. Univ., Dir. of the Univ. Infectious Disease Laboratory; b. 1878 in Tokyo, 3rd son of the late Baron Nagayo, father of sanitary work in Japan; grad. from Med. Coll., above institution, class '04, and further studied at the Univ. Hall and in Germany till '09; has been connected with his alma mater ever since with a short break in '06-07 when he was attached to the Bodyguard Division; attended the Tropical Disease Conference at Batavia, '21. Add. Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 645](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n645/mode/1up))

**Nukada, Yutaka** M.D., Dir. of Nukada Hospital & also of Teikoku Female Medical College (private); b. 1880 in Okayama-ken; edu. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '60; served a term of assist. in the Internal Med. Dept., Univ. Hospital; further studied in Munchen, '07-09; re-entered his alma mater to conduct special researches, and the thesis he submitted soon after got him the degree in '13. Add. Akasaka Hikawa-cho, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Ogata, Masakiyo** M.D., Dir. of Ogata Hospital of Women's Disease; b. 1864 Ehime-ken; studied medicine at Tokyo Imp. Univ., then at Berlin Univ. and elsewhere, '88-92; Chief of Obstetrics Dept. of Ogata Hospital (his adopted father's); started his own, '02; was Chairman of Osaka Medical Practitioner's Ass'n. and also of Osaka Midwives Ass'n., etc. Add. Imabashi, Higashi-ku, Osaka. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Okada, Waichiro** M.D., Prof. of Otorhinolaryngology at Tokyo Imp. Univ., Pres. Otorhinolar. Society of Japan; b. 1864 in Ehime-ken; grad. from Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '89; after serving a term of assist. at his alma mater was sent to Germany and Austria to study his speciality, '96-99; prof. at his alma mater since '02; attended the International Medical Conference in America in '22. Add. Koj. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Okamoto, Yanamatsu** M.D., Emeritus Prof. at Medical Dept. of Kyoto Imp. Univ.; b. 1863 in Hyogo-ken; grad. from Med. Coll. of Tokyo Imp. Univ., '89; further studied in Germany. Add. Yoshida-machi, Kyoto. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Okamura, Tatsuhiko** M.D., Dermatologist, Dir. of Okamura Hospital; b. 1870 in Tokyo; edu. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '95; after serving a term of assist. at the alma mater, further studied his speciality at Freiburg and Vienna, '96-00; established the present hospital in '00. Add. Kanda Nishiogawa-machi, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Okonogi, Shinrokuro** Dir. of Okonogi Otological Hospital; b. 1860 in Fukushima-ken; first studied at Tokyo Imp. Univ.; then went to Germany for study, '88-94; established his own hospital in '96. Add. Hongo Moto-machi, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Saiki, Nori** M.D., Ph. D. & M. Sc. (Yale), Dietetist, Dir. of the Dietetic Laboratory, Home Office; b. 1876 in Ehime-ken; grad. from the Med. Dept., Third High School, class '98; further studied at Yale, '06-08; served as Assis. at his alma mater, '98-99; assisted his father's practice in the country, '99-01; assist. in the Med. Chemico Class-room, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '01-02; in the Infectious Disease Institute, Home Office, '02. Add. Govt. Dietetic Laboratory, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Sakaki, Junjiro** M.D., Dir. & Prop. of Sakaki Hospital for Women's Diseases, Tokyo; b. 1859 in Tokyo; grad. Med. Tokyo Imp. Univ., then studied his speciality at Berlin. Add. Koj. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Masakichi** M.D., Adviser to the Sasaki Hospital, Tokyo; b. 1856; grad. from Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '78; further studied in Germany, '79-83; appointed on his return Prof. at his alma mater and Dean of the College of Medicine; later resigned the post to devote himself to practice. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Sasaki, Taka-oki** (or Ryuko), M.D., Mem. of German Chemical Society & of Berlin Physiological Society, Pres. of Kyoundo (private) Hospital, Tokyo; b. 1878 in Tokyo; edu. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '02, and further studied at the post-grad. course and at Strasburg, Berlin, etc., '05-10; filled chair of Internal Medicine at Tokyo Imp. Univ., '13-16; resigning the post in '17 has since devoted himself to his Hospital. Add Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 646](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n646/mode/1up))

**Sassa, Rempei** M.D., Chief of the Heart and Kidney Dept., Kyoundo Hospital (private), Tokyo; b. 1882 in Okayama-ken; took license in '00; grad. from Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; assistant to Dr. Kumakawa, his teacher, '00-11; studied at Munchen, Vienna and Halle, '11-14. Add. Kyoundo Hospital, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Sata, Yasuhiko** Dr. Med. (Japan), Pathologist & Pathological Anatomist, Pres. Osaka Pref. Medical College; b. 1871 in Kagoshima; grad. from Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; further studied in Germany, '97-00; appointed on the staff of the Osaka Medical School, '94; then its director, '02, Lecturer at the Med. Coll., Kyoto Imp. Univ. as additional post, '04; toured through Europe and America by Government order, '12-13; appointed Dir. and Prof. of the Osaka Pref. Med. Coll. and Pres. of the University Hospital, '19; represented Japan at the International Tuberculosis Conference in Naples, '00, and was recommended its hon. president. Add. Dojima, Kita-ku, Osaka. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Sata, Yoshihisa** M.D., Dir. of Sata Nerve Hospital (private); b. 1886 in Kagoshima; edu. at Chiba Medical School, '11; served at the Yamada Nerve Hospital (Tokyo) till '23; set up his own hospital; granted the degree in '23 by the Tokyo Imp. Univ., by submitting a thesis. Add. Shiba Nishinokubo, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Sato, Sankichi** M.D., Crown Mem. House of Peers since '22; Emeritus Prof. at Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1857 in Gifu-ken; learned mineralogy at the Tokyo Kaisei Gakko, but afterward took to Medicine which he finished in '82; studied in Germany, '83-87; appoint. Prof. of his alma mater on return. Add. Kanda Sarugaku-cho, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Sato, Sei-ichiro** M.D., Dir. of Surgical Dept., Juntendo (private) Hospital; b. 1883 in Tokyo; grad. from the Fukuoka Med. Coll. affiliated to the Kyoto Imp. Univ., '90; was for a while assistant at the Fukuoka Coll.; after returning home continued his study at the alma mater; entered the Juntendo Hospital, '15. Add. Juntendo Hospital, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Sato, Tatsujiro** M.D., Pres. of Juntendo Hospital (private), 2nd Baron, being the adopted son of late Baron Susumu Sato, founder of the Hospital and a noted surgeon of the day; b. 1868 in Wakasa; edu. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '97; further studied in Germany, '97-00, and in Austria, '03-04; succeeded to the adopted father's duty as Pres. of the Hospital on his death in '21; also acts as Dir. of Tokyo Coll. of Medicine since '81. Add. Juntendo Hospital, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Sato, Tsunemaru** M.D., Surg.-Gen. (ret), Chief Physician-in-Ordinary to the Imperial Household; b. 1872 in Nagoya; edu. at Coll. Med., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '96, and post-grad. course, '98; sent by Govt. to study at Berlin, Munchen, etc., '07-10; entered the Army in '97 as Surg. Sub-Lieut.; Surg.-Maj., '04; Resident Surgeon in Germany, '07-10; Surg.-Col., '12; Maj.-Gen., '17; Surg.-Gen., '22; Pres. of Japan Red Cross Hospital, '21-27; present post since '27. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Segawa, Masago** M.D., a specialist in Children's Diseases, Dir. of Segawa Pediatric Hospital; b. 1884 in Sendai; edu. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '09; assist. at the alma mater, '09-13; proceeded to Berlin to further study his speciality, but had to return in '14 owing to the outbreak of the War. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Shiga, Kiyoshi** M.D., noted Bacteriologist, Dir., Govt. Hospital of Chosen & Pres. Seoul Medical Coll. since '20; b. 1870 in Sendai; grad. from Medicine, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '96; entered the Infectious Diseases Investigation Laboratory; its Section Chief, '99; studied further in Germany, '01-04; sent to India by the Government '09, and to Italy and England '12; entered Dr. Kitazato's Laboratory in '14, later combining a chair of bacteriology at the Med. Coll., Keio Univ. Add. Seoul. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 647](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n647/mode/1up))

**Shimazono, Junjiro** M.D., Prof. of Internal Med., Tokyo Imp. Univ., Pres. of Internal Med. Society of Japan, etc.; b. 1879 in Wakayama-ken; edu. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '04; one-year volunteer in the Army in '04 and as Surg.-Lieutenant went through active service in the Russo-Japanese War; placed on the reserve list, '07; assist. at the alma mater, '07-11; stayed in Germany studying his speciality, '11-13; Prof. at Okayama Medical Coll., '13; Prof. at Kyoto Imp. Univ., '16-26. Add. Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Shiota, Hiroshige** M.D., Prof. of Surgery at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. and Chief Surgeon of the Univ. Hospital; b. 1873 in Kyoto-fu; grad. from above Univ., class '99; assist. at the alma mater till '07; studied his speciality at Vienna, '07-09; Prof. at the alma mater since '09; elected Pres. of the Japan Surgical Society, '19. Add. Hongo Yumi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Takagi, Kikan** Baron (2nd), M.D., Mem. House of Peers, Prof. Tokyo Jikeikai Medical Coll.; b. 1874, son of the late Surgeon-Gen. Takagi Kenkan; studied medicine in England. Add. Azabu Higashi-Toriizaka, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Takata, Koan** M.D., Prop. of Takata Hospital (Tokyo) and Nan-ko-in Hospital (Chigasaki); b. 1861 in Kyoto; grad. from Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; subsequently commenced practice, lung troubles being his speciality. Add. Kojimachi Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Tashiro, Yoshinori** M.D., Prof. at Med. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1864; grad. from the Med. Coll., '89; a chair at his alma mater, '92; established the Tashiro Hospital, '95; was for a time in charge of the Mitsui Charity Hospital. Add. Shitaya Neribei-cho, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Tawara, Yoshizumi** M.D., Mem. of Japan Pharmacopœia Com., Home Office, etc.; b. 1855 in Saga-ken; grad. from Tokyo Imp. Univ., (Pharmacy) class '81; further studied his speciality by Govt. order at Berlin, Heidelberg, etc., '90-93; has devoted himself to pharmaceutical affairs as expert of the Home Office or member of institution in this line; Dir. of the Sanitary Laboratory, Home Office, '87-23. Add. Kyobashi Echizen-bori, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Yoshimoto, Seitaro** M.D., Dir. and Chief of Internal Division of Japan Red Cross Hospital; b. 1873 in Fukuoka-ken; edu. at Med Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '97; assist. to Dr. Prof. Aoyama, head of the Internal Dept., Univ. Hospital, '97-02; physician of the Red Cross Hospital, '03; sent to Germany to study Pathology and Pathological chemistry at Berlin, '06-09; served in the Russo-Japanese War attached to the Red Cross Medical Corps. Add. Japan Red Cross Hospital, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Yoshioka, Yayoi** a lady physician, Principal of the Tokyo Female Medical Coll., Pres. of Tokyo Shisei Hospital; b. 1871 in Shizuoka-ken; grad. from the former Saisei Gakusha (a private medical school); established the Medical Coll. in '04 and became its Principal. Add. Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

**Yukawa, Gen-yo** M.D. (Japan and Germany), Dir. of Yukawa Gastro-enteric Hospital (private), Osaka; b. 1867 in Wakayama; grad. from the Kyoto Prefectural School of Med., class '90; further studied at the Elective course, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '91-93, and lastly in Germany, '00-02, specializing in digestive organs; meanwhile practised in Wakayama and also served at the Nagayo Gastro-enteric Hospital; established his hospital in Osaka on returning home. Add. Imabashi Sanchome, Osaka. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 648](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n648/mode/1up))

### Mining

**Abe, Goichi** Chairman of the Board of Dir. of the Ibaraki Smokeless Coal Co., Ltd., Karatsu Mining Co., Ltd., etc.; b. 1873 in Gifu-ken. Has risen to the present position through connection with Mr. S. Asano's business. Add. Kyobashi, Tokyo., — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Furukawa, Toranosuke** Baron (cr. '15), Pres. of the Furukawa Mining Co., son of the late Ichibei Furukawa and inherited his father's immense properties including more than twenty mines; b. 1882; edu. in Mining at Columbia, U.S.A., class '07. Add. Nihombashi Setomono-cho, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Okura, Kishichiro** 2nd Baron (suc. '26), Pres. of Okura Mining Co., Dir. of Okura Trading Co., Akita Timber Mfg. Co., Rly. Car. Mfg. Co., etc; b. 1882; first son of late Okura Kihachiro; studied at Cambrdige and at other institutions in London; returned home '06 to devote himself to his father's business. Add. Azabu Hiro-o, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Saka, Nakasuke** Chairman of Board of Dirs. Fujita Mining Co., Fujita Gumi, etc.; b. 1879 in Yamaguchi-ken; passed higher civil service examination, '95; entered the Home Dept., '01, being promoted to Personal Secy. to the Home Minister; served at local government, '04-08; Governor of Ishikawa, etc.; entered Sumitomo Firm, '16. Add. Nizaimon-cho, Osaka. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Tsunesaburo** Dir. of Furukawa Mining Co., Nihon Elec. Wire Co.; b. 1873 in Oita-ken; grad. at Keio Univ., '96; entered Furukawa Mining Co.; went to U.S.A. and studied at Harvard Univ., meanwhile observing mining industry there, '03-06; visited Europe, '12. Add. Nakano-machi, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Ginnosuke** Dir. of Tanaka Mining Co., Auditor of the Taihoku Savings Bank; b. 1873 in Tokyo, grandson of the late Heihachi Tanaka, widely known as “Raw-silk-king” in his day; studied at Trinity Hall, Camb., England; is a well-known figure in social circles. Add. Azabu Ichibei-cho, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Buntaro** Dr. Eng., Dir. of Mitsui Mining Co.; b. 1861 in Fukui-ken; grad. from Tokyo Imp. Univ., '86; further studied in Germany, '89-91; returning home, expert to Ikuno Mine and next Dir. of Kamioka Mine owned by the Mitsui. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Yamada, Naoya** Dr. Eng., Dir. of Mitsui Mining Co. and Hokkaido Colliery & S.S. Co.; b. 1860 in Tokyo; grad. from Mining and Metallurgy at Tokyo Imp. Univ., '85; was Mining Supervisor before he entered the Mitsui. Add. Koj. Nakarokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Yamaguchi, Kisaburo** Ph. D., Mng.-Dir. of Furukawa Mining Co. and Dir. of Furukawa Trading Co., Asahi Denka Co., etc.; b. 1874 in Tokyo; grad. from Johns Hopkins, '02. Add. Shinagawa Higashi-dai, near Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Yasukawa, Kei-ichiro** Baron (cr. '19), highest tax-payer, mine-owner, Dir. of several cos.; b. 1819 in Fukuoka-ken; studied at Keio-gijuku, '72; has made a fortune in coal-mining; founded single-handed the Meiji Semmon College in '08 by contributing ¥3,000,000, and has been duly rewarded (the institution being transferred to the Govt. with all its properties in 1920). Add. Wakamatsu, Fukuoka. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

### Politics

**Abe, Isoh** M.P., Lecturer at Waseda Univ.; b. 1865 in Fukuoka-ken; studied in the Doshisha (Kyoto), class '84; then a pastor for some years; studied at Theological Seminary at Hartford, '91-94, and at Glasgow; Principal of the Doshisha Middle School for some years; Prof. at Waseda Univ., '04-27, and then lecturer; founded the Japan Febian Society in '24; organized in '26 with others the Social Democratic Party of which he is leader; visited U.S.A. and Europe in '29; is an authority on social politics and local administration. Works: Interpretation of Social Questions, Municipal Monopolies, etc. Add. Zoshigaya, near Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 649](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n649/mode/1up))

**Adachi, Kenzo** M.P., Minister of Home Affairs, a leader of the Minsei Party; b. 1864 in Kumamoto; went to Korea and established several newspapers to promote the Japanese interest in the peninsula and was involved in the Korean Queen assassination affair but acquitted on trial; Parl. Senior Under-Sec. of Foreign Department in the Okuma Cabinet; made a tour through Europe and America, '19; Minister of Communications, '25-27; present post in July '29. Add. Azabu Hiroo-machi, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Aoki, Nobumitsu** Visc., Mem. House of Peers since 1897; b. 1867, 2nd son of late I. Nakayama, of Mito clan and adopted by the Aoki family which was the petty daimiate at Asada, Settsu; was auditor of the Bank of Japan till '25; took active part in the formation of Kiyoura Cabinet. Add. Ushigome Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Aoyama, Yukinobu** Visc., Mem. House of Peers where he is a leader of the Kenkyukai, the most powerful group; is also on the Board of Directors of the Fifteenth Bank and other concerns; b. 1861, a ninth descendant of a small daimiate in Mino; has a seat in the Upper House since 1890; is Manager of the Tokyo Rifle Society. Add. Koj. Fujimi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Arai, Kentaro** Privy Councillor, Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1863 in Niigata; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ., in law, '90; entered the Treasury in which he rose to the position of Vice-Minister in '06; Dir. of Finance Dept. of Korean Govt.-Gen.; was Min. of Agr. & Com. in the Kato (Adm.) cabinet in 1922; then present post in '26. Add. Koishikawa Kanatomi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Den, Kenjiro** Baron (cr. '07), Mem. House of Peers, Chairman of Japan Electric Society, etc.; b. 1855 in Hyogo-ken; held at several places post of local chief Police Inspector; Vice-Min. of Communications, '98; President of the Kansai R'ly Co., at the same time M.P. for Hyogo-ken; Vice-Min. of Communications, '00-06; Min. of same, '16-17; Gov.-Gen. of Formosa, '18-20; Min. of Agr. and Com., '22-23. Add. Tamagawa Kaminoge, near Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Egi, Yoku** Minister of Railways, Mem. House of Peers (nom. '16); b. 1873 in Yamaguchi-ken; grad. Law Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '97, and studied at Univ. Hall; Sec. Chief of Colonial Bureau, '10-13; Chief Secy. of 2nd Okuma Cabinet, '14-16; same post in the Kato (Visc.) Cabinet, '24-25; Justice Minister, '25-27; is Adviser to the Minsei-to party; present post in July, '29. Add. Akasaka Omote-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Fujimura, Yoshiro** Baron, Mem. House of Peers, Bar of Middle Tem., ex-Sect. Chief of the Mitsui Bussan Co.; b. 1870 in Kumamoto; edu. in Law at Cambridge, returning home '03, and entered the Mitsui; its London agent till '10; removed to the head office, but left it soon after; was Minister of Foreign Affairs and then of Communications in the Kiyoura cabinet, '24. Add. Nakano-machi, near Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Shiro** Mem. House of Peers; b. 1861 in Tokyo; attache to Japanese Legation in Vienna, 1886; then in Berlin; afterwards Private Secretary to the Minister of Communication and of Agriculture & Commerce, &c.; Vice-Minister of Agriculture & Commerce, '98; now Pres. of Taiwan Sugar Co. and other concerns. Add. Shiba Mita, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Funakoshi, Mitsunojo** 2nd Baron, Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1867 in Hiroshima; studied in Germany, '86-93; passed Diplomatic Service Examination in '94; Eleve Consul at Vladivostok the same year; at San Francisco in '95; Legation Secy. at Washington in '97; to London in '99; Secretary at head office, '02; Secy. at Vienna in '03; at Berlin, '06-09; then at head office and lastly Emb. Counsellor at Berlin, '12-14. Add. Ushigome Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 650](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n650/mode/1up))

**Futa-ara, Yoshinori** 1st Count; b. 1886, of the house of Date, formerly a daimyo, and was adopted by the family; studied law at Tokyo Imp. Univ.; Secretary to a local Govt. for a time; was on the suite of the Crown Prince when he visited England in '21. Lady Futa-ara is sister to the late Prince Kitashirakawa. Add. Yotsuya Kasumiga-oka, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Hamada, Kunimatsu** M.P. & Parl. Vice-Minister of Justice Dept. ('27-29); b. 1868 in Miye-ken; grad. from Chuo Univ.; returned to the House of Representatives thrice from Miye-ken and was once Vice-Pres. of the House. Add. Koishikawa Otsukanaka-machi, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Hamaguchi, Yuko** Premier & Pres. of the Minsei Party, M.P.; b. 1870 in Kochi; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '95; Dir. Tobacco Monopoly Bureau, '07-12; Vice-Min. of Communications, '12-13; Vice-Min. of Finance in 2nd Okuma Cabinet, '14-15; Parl. Jun. Secy. to Finance Dept. for a while in '15; Minister of Finance in '24; Minister of Home Affairs, '26-27. Add. Zoshigaya, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Hara, Kado** Lawyer, D.L., ex-Min. of Justice; b. 1867 in Nagano-ken; grad. at Tokyo University, '89; Counsellor of Agr. & Com. Dept. in '09; Chief of the Tokyo Mine Inspection Office and that of Osaka, '01-03; then opened law office; Min. of Justice, '27-29. Add. Koj. Iida-machi, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Hayakawa, Tetsuya** ex-M.P.; b. 1865 in Okayama; grad. at Sapporo Agr. Coll., '84; studied law in U.S.A. and Germany; entered the diplomatic service, '85 and attache at Seoul Legation until '88; to Consulate at San Francisco; to the Legation at Berlin, '89-92; Private Secy. to the Minister of Agriculture, '92-97; Minister Resident, '97-98; Secy. of the Cabinet, '98; Dir. of the Political Bureau, Foreign Office, in the 1st Okuma Cabinet; succeeded in procuring foreign loan for the Hokkaido Colliery Co. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Higuchi, Hideo** M.P.; b. 1875 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Litt. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., '00; was lecturer at the Shinshu Univ. and the Bukkyo Univ. Add. Nippori, near Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Hirosawa, Kinjiro** Count, Mem. House of Peers; b. 1871 at Yamaguchi; proceeded to England for study '86; Private Secretary to the Premier; Min. to Spain and Portugal, '21-24. He is a son of the celebrated State Councillor Hirosawa who took active part in the work of Restoration. Add. Azabu Zaimoku-cho, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Hoshijima, Jiro** M.P., social reformer and journalist; b. 1887 in Okayama-ken; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '17; Mng. of Y.M.S. of said Univ., and Dir. of Central Legal Advisory Council. Add. Hongo Masago-cho, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Inukai, Ki** M.P. of unbroken record, ex-Minister of Communications; leader of the former Nationalist Party (dissolved in '22) and then that of the Kaku-shin Club (merged in Seiyu-kai in '25); b. 1855 at Okayama; early began political career; was editor of the “Hochi Shimbun” till the opening of Imperial Diet, '90; Minister of Education for a short while in the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet in '98; Min. of Com'tions, '24-25. Add. Yotsuya Shinano-machi, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Inuzuka, Katsutaro** Crown Mem. House of Peers; ex-Vice-Minister of Agr. & Com.; b. 1868 in Tokyo; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '89; Per. Sec. to Home Minister, '90; Sec. of Communication Dept., '92; Dir. of R'ly Bureau, '99; Dir. of Public Works Bureau, '01; Pref. Governor, '10-12; Vice-Min. of Communications, '13-14; Japanese Delegate in the Inter. Labor Conference in '22. Add. Kami-Osaki Chojamaru, near Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 651](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n651/mode/1up))

**Ishii, Kikujiro** Visc. (created Baron '12; Visc. '16), Privy Councillor, Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '16); b. 1866 at Chiba; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; attache to Paris Legation, '91; 3rd Legation Sec., '93; Consul at Jinsen, '96; 2nd and then 1st Sec. at Peking Legation during the Boxer trouble; Sec. at the head office and Chief of Telegraph Section, '00; Director of Commerce Bureau, '04; despatched to San Francisco and Vancouver in '07 in connection with the anti-Japanese riot there; Vice-Min. of For. Affairs, '08; Amb. to Paris; Min. of Foreign Affairs, '15-16; Special Envoy to U.S.A. in '17; Amb. to Paris, '20-27; present post in '29. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Ishizuka, Eizo** Mem. House of Peers; b. 1865 at Aizu; appointed Legal Adviser to the Korean Govt. in '95; next held the post of Chief Counsellor to the Government-Gen. of Formosa; then Civil Administrator of the Occupied Districts in Manchuria, '05; Chief Counsellor of Chosen Govt. in '07; Dir. of Com., Agr. & Ind. Dept., Korea, in '12; Pres. Oriental Development Co., '16-24. Add. Ushigome Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Ito, Miyoji** Count (cr. Baron '95; Visc. '07; Count '22), Privy Councillor; b. 1857 at Nagasaki; started his career in civil service from the lowest ladder; the late Prince Ito's Private Secretary, '90-95; Chief Secy. of the Cabinet also under the Prince; Minister of Agriculture & Commerce in '98 in the Ito-Itagaki Cabinet; Privy Councillor in '99; on the occasion of the Japan-China War he was sent to Chefoo to exchange the Signed Treaty of Peace; was conspicuous for causing the fall of the Wakatsuki Cabinet in '27. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Izawa, Takio** Mem. House of Peers, ex-Mayor of Tokyo; b. 1869 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ.; Metropolitan Police Inspector, '00; Governor of Wakayama, '07; Gov. of Ehime, '08-12; Gov. of Niigata, '12-14; Metropolitan Police Superintendent, '14-15; Gov.-Gen. of Formosa, '24-26; Mayor of Tokyo, '26. Add. Sugamo Miyanaka, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Kamada, Eikichi** Privy Councillor (since '27), ex-Pres. of Keio Univ., etc.; b. 1858 in Kii, for which he sat once in the House of Representatives, '95; studied at Keio, '74-76; Principal of Kagoshima Zoshikan High School, '81-83; Dir. of Oita Middle School; but returning once more to his alma mater in '89 toured abroad to inspect educational affairs in Europe and America; Pres. of Keio on returning home; represented Japanese Govt. in the International Labor Conference at Washington, '19; Min. of Education in the Kato (Adm.) Cabinet, '22. Add. Meguro, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Kanda, Masao** M.P.; b. 1878 in Tochigi; edu. at Waseda Semmon Gakko (now univ.), class '01; served as teacher in Szechuan, China, for a number of years; special correspondent of the Asahi in Peking, '07-15; on the editorial staff of the Asahi till '24. Add. Mejiro Bunka-mura, near Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Kaneko, Kentaro** Viscount (Baron '95; Viscount '07), Privy Councillor, Hon. L.L.D. (Harvard); ex-Pres. of America's Friends Society and Japan-American Association; b. 1853 in Fukuoka; grad. from Harvard, U.S.A., class '78; private Secretary to the late Prince Ito as Premier, '85-86, and Pres. of the Privy Council, '88-90; Chief Secretary of the House of Peers; Vice-Min. of Agr. & Com., '94-96; full Minister, '98; Minister of Justice, '00-01. On the outbreak of the Russo-Japanese War he was sent to U.S.A. to represent Japan unofficially. Add. Kojimachi Ichibancho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Kanno, Katsunosuke** Mem. House of Peers (nom. '22); b. 1867 in Gifu-ken; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '96; passed the higher civil service examination the following year; secretary of Agr. & Com., and next a similar post in Treasury; attached to the Embassy in London, '07; Dir. Printing Bureau; Dir. of the Finance Bureau; Vice-Min. of Finance, '18-22. Add. Koj. Fujimicho, Tokyo. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 652](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n652/mode/1up))

**Kano, Jigoro** Crown Mem. House of Peers (since '22), ex-Pres. of Tokyo Higher Normal School; b. 1860 in Kumamoto-ken; Dir. and Prof. of the Peers' School, '83; Director of the Special Education Bureau, '98; is a patron of athletic sports having led the Japanese lads that attended the Olympics in Paris. Add. Koishikawa Otsuka Sakashita, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kataoka, Naoharu** M.P., Adviser to the Minsei Party; b. 1859 in Kochi-ken; grad. at the Toyo Gakko; entered civil service as a district administrative official; officer of the Nippon Life Ins. Co. and later its Pres. which post he resigned in '18; was a delegate to the 5th International Actuaries Conference in '06 and elected its Vice-Pres.; Parl. Vice-Minister of Home Affairs, '24-25; Minister of Commerce & Industry, '25; Finance Minister, '26-27. Add. Waseda Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kawamura, Takeji** ex-Gov.-Gen. of Taiwan, Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1871 in Akita-ken; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '97; passed Higher Civil Serv. Exam.; entered the service of Dept of Communications; transferred to Home Office, then to Formosan Govt.; attended the 6th International Post Conference in Rome; Dir. of Police Affairs Bureau, '19-21; Pres. of Colonization Bureau, '21; Vice-Min. of Home Affairs and next Pres. of South Manchuria R'ly, '22-24; Gov.-Gen. of Formosa, '27-29. Add. Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kikuchi, Kenjiro** M.P. from Mito City; b. 1867 at Mito City; grad. from Lit. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '93; Prof. of the Yamaguchi High School; went to China and became Superintendent of the Tung Wen College in Shanghai; then General Superintendent of the Normal College in Nanking; after returning from China was Principal of the Mito Middle School for some years. Add. Mito City. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kiyose, Ichiro** D.L., Vice-president of House of Representatives, M.P. for Osaka; b. 1884 in Hyogo-ken; edu. at Kyoto Imp. Univ., in Law, class '08, and further studied in England and elsewhere. Add. Dojima Hamadori, Osaka. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kiyoura, Keigo** Visc. (cr. 1912), ex-Premier; b. 1850 at Kumamoto; first was a school master; then a humble clerk in Saitama prefectural office; Public Procurator, '73; next Secy of Police Bureau, Home Office; Vice-Minister of Justice in quick succession; Minister of Justice, '98-00 and '01-03; Minister of Agr. & Com., '03-06; Privy Councillor, '06; Vice-Pres. of Privy Council, '17-22; Pres. of Privy Council, '22-24; Premier, '24. Add. Omori, near Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Koba, Sadanaga** D.L., Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1859; studied in Germany and Austria, '82-85; Private Secretary to the Minister of Education, &c., till '95; next Dir. of Ordinary Edu. Bureau, Chief Counsellor and Vice-Min. of Education, '03-05. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kobashi, Ichita** Min. of Education and Minsei-to M.P.; b. 1870 in Kumamoto; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '98; Counsellor of Yamaguchi-ken, then of Nagasaki-ken; entered Home Dept. as Secy.; then became Counsellor; sent abroad; Vice-Min. of Home Affairs, '1[illegible]-22; Chief Secy. of Cabinet, '24; present post in July '29. Add .Kami-Osaki, near Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kodama, Hideo** 2nd Count, Administrative Superintendent of Chosen, Mem. House of Peers (since '18); b. 1876 in Tokyo; eldest son of the late General Kodama, Chief of Gen. Staff at the time of the Japan-Russia War; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., in '00 and entered the service of Korean Govt.-Gen., in which he rose to Chief of Gen. Affairs, '12; transferred to Home Govt. as Chief Secretary of Cabinet, '16; Dir. Bureau of Decoration, '18-21; Gov.-Gen. of Kwantung, '24-27; present post in June '29. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 653](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n653/mode/1up))

**Kodera, Kenkichi** M.P.; b. 1877 in Kobe; edu. at Kobe Com. School, Columbia & Johns Hopkins Universities; and further in Germany, etc.; went through one-year volunteer service and served in the '04-05 war; has founded and maintains at his expense a middle school at Sanda, Hyogo-ken. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Koizumi, Matajiro** Minister of Communications and M.P.; b. 1865 in Kanagawa-ken; took to journalism as Editor of the Yokohama Boeki Shimbun; was mem. of the Kanagawa Pref. Assembly; became Min. of Communications on the formation of Hamaguchi Cabinet in July '29; sits in the Lower House since 1912 as a Minsei-to M.P. Add. Shiba Isarago-cho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Koizumi, Sakutaro** M.P.; b. 1872 in Shizuoka-ken; Vice-Pres. of the House of Representatives, '24-27; has been connected with various papers in the country and also sat in Tokyo Municipal Council, etc. Add. Azabu Hiroo-machi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Kojima, Kazuo** ex-M.P.; b. 186[illegible] in Hyogo-ken; formerly a journalist and chief editor of the Yorozu Choho; was returned to the Lower House six times from Tokyo City. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Komatsu, Kenjiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1863 in Nagano-ken; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '88; entered the Department of Communications for which he was Vice-Min. in '11, but resigned in '12; Min. of R'lys in the Kiyoura Cabinet. Add. Shiba Kuruma-cho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Kuhara, Fusanosuke** M.P., ex-Min. of Communications, formerly Pres. of Kuhara Company & lessee of the Hidachi copper mine, etc.; b. 1869 in Osaka; grad. at Keio Gijuku, '89; a clerk at Kosaka copper mine which was resuscitated by him from the threatened danger of decay; was superviser of the mine till '98; bought Hidachi mine, '05; and has made it one of the best copper mines in Japan; sent to Germany and Russia by the Govt. on semi-official mission, Oct. '27; retired from business and took to politics joining the Seiyukai party, '28; entered the cabinet occupying the portfolio of communications under Premier Tanaka, '28. Add. Nakano-shima, Osaka. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Matsuda, Genji** Minister of Overseas Affairs and M.P.; b. 1878 in Oita; edu. at a private law school in Tokyo which he grad. in '96; passed same year High. Civil Service and Judicial Service examinations; sat on the Bench two years and then started practice in Tokyo; once a whip of the Seiyu-kai; Senior Sec. of Home Office, '20-21; Vice-Chairman of Lower House, '22; became Minister of Overseas Affairs on the formation of the Hamaguchi Ministry in July '29; is a leader of Minsei Party. Add. Koj. Cobancho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Matsuoka, Kimpei** 2nd Baron, Mem. House of Peers; b. 1876 in Tokyo; edu. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '00; further studied Political Economy at the post-grad. course; after teaching at the alma mater for two years as Assist.-Prof., proceeded to Europe in '05, returning home in '09 after studying Economics in England; resumed duty at the Univ., '19; Japanese delegate at the International Seamens' Conference at Genoa in '20. Add. Azabu Kasumi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Minami, Hiroshi** Mem. House of Peers; b. 1869 in Toyama-ken; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '06; Secretary of the Cabinet and then chief Secretary, '08; Governor of Fukuoka; Vice-Min. of Education, '18-22. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Minoura, Katsundo** ex-M.P.; b. 1854 in Oita-ken; began political life in early part of Meiji era under the then Count Okuma; three times entered the Govt. service first as Dir. Com. Bureau, Dept. of Agr. & Com. in '97, second as Vice-Min. of Communications in '98; was once elected Vice-Pres. of the House of Representatives; Min. of Communications, '15-16; was for a long while Manager of the Hochi Shimbun; was implicated in the Osaka Licensed Quarter Scandal in 1926-27. Add. Ushigome Higashi-gokencho, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 654](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n654/mode/1up))

**Mitsuchi, Chuzo** M.P., ex-Min. of Finance; b. 1871 in Kagawa-ken; grad. from Tokyo Higher Normal Sch. and further studied in England and Germany; Prof. at his alma mater; educational adviser of the former Korean Govt.; later took to politics and was repeatedly returned M.P., Sec. to the Education Min. and then Chief Sec. of the Cabinet under the Seiyukai Administration; then Parl. Vice-Min. of Agr. & Forestry; Minister of Education, '27 and then Min. of Finance, till June '29. Add. Aoyama Kitamachi, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Mizuno, Rentaro** D.C.L., ex-Minister of Education, Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1868 in Akita; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., in '92; entered Home Office, '95; sent abroad '96 to investigate copy-right affairs and attended as Japanese delegate at World's Conference for the Protection of Right of Industrial Property held at Brussels; framed the draft of the Copyright Law; Dir. of Public Works Bureau, '10-12; Vice-Minister of Home Office, '13-14; full Minister, '16-18; Civil Administrator, Korea, '20-22; Home Minister, '19-23; Education Minister, '27-28. Add. Shiba Shirokane-Sarumachi, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Mochizuki, Keisuke** M.P., ex-Min. of Communications ('27-29), mine-owner in Hiroshima; b. 1867 in Hiroshima Pref.; was returned to the House of Representatives eight times from his native place; is one of the leading members of the Seiyukai party. Add. Shinagawa-machi, near Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Motoda, Hajime** M.P. of unbroken record, Lawyer; b. 1856 in Oita; was a leader of the National Association organized in '92, as organ of the clan statesmen; joined late Prince Ito when he organized the Seiyukai in '00; Vice-President of the House of Rep., '98-02; Dir. of Colonization Bureau, '11; Minister of Communications, '13-14; Min. of Railways, '18-21; Pres. of the Lower House, '28-29. Add. Kojimachi Kioicho, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Nagai, Ryutaro** M.P. and Parl. Vice-Min. of Foreign Office; b. 1881 at Kanazawa; grad. from Waseda (politics) in '05, and further studied in England and Germany, '06-09; Prof. at Waseda Univ.; Parl. Counsellor of For. Office, '26-27; the present post in July '29. Add. Totsuka Tobichi, near Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Nagata, Hidejiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '23); b. 1876 in Hyogo-ken; studied at the 1st High Sch.; passed the Exam. for the Bar; Principal of a middle school and local Educational Inspector; entered Home Office; Prof. at a Govt. School for a time; Dir. Police Bureau, '13-18; Deputy Mayor of Tokyo, '21-23; Mayor, '23-24. Add. Zoshigaya,Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Nakahashi, Tokugoro** M.P., ex-Min. of Com. & Ind., Pres. of Uji-gawa Electric and Japan Lime Nitrogen Cos., Auditor of South Manchuria Rly, and Japan-China S.S. Cos.; b. 1860 at Kanazawa; grad. from Tokyo Imp. Univ., '84; was an official in the Com. Dept. where he occupied the post of Director of the Railway Bureau; resigned about '98 to take charge of Osaka Shosen Kaisha of which he was president till 1916; elected M.P. for Kanazawa city, '17; Min. of Education, '18-22; Min. of Com. & Ind., '27-29. Add. Koj. Nakarokubancho, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Nakamura, Takashi** M.P. from Wakayama-ken; grad. from Tokyo Higher Com. School and passed Diplomatic & Consular Service Exam., '06; Consul-General at New York, '17; Director of the Com. Affairs Bureau, Foreign Office; then Ambassador to Argentina and later Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs. Add. Ushigome Suido-cho, Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 655](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n655/mode/1up))

**Nakano, Seigo** M.P., Parl. Vice-Min. of Communications Dept.; b. 1885 in Fukuoka-ken; edu. at Waseda in Politics, class '09; connected with the Tokyo, Asahi, resigning which he started the Oriental Review; present post on the formation of the Minseito Cabinet under Premier Hamaguchi in July '29; is one of the prominent members of the Minsei party. Works: The World Politics, At the Seat of the Peace Conference, etc. Add. Sendagaya Harajuku, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Nakano, Torakichi** M.P.; b. 1879 in Fukushima-ken; grad. from Law, Waseda Univ., '01; Police-Inspector in Hokkaido, Formosa, and Korea. Add. Fukushima. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Nishikubo, Hiromichi** ex-Mayor of Tokyo, Mem. House of Peers (nom. '16); b. 1863 at Saga; grad. from the now defunct Govt. Law School; Prof. at Military Staff College; sent to England in '02; Prefectural Governor till '14; Superintendent of Metropolitan Police Board, '15-16; Mayor of Tokyo, '27. Add. Ichikawa-machi, Chiba-ken. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Ogasawara, Chokan** Count, Mem. House of Peers; b. 1885 of a former Daimyo family; studied at the Peers' Sch. and then at Cambridge; was once in the service of the Imp. Household; was Senior Counsellor to Navy Office and then Dir. Census Board till it was abolished in '23. Add. Ushigome Ichigaya Kawada-cho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Ogawa, Gotaro** D.L., M.P., Parl. Vice-Min. of Finance Dept. since July '29; b. 1876 in Okayama-ken; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ., Law, class '03; Lecturer at Kyoto Imp. Univ. till '07; further studied Finance in Germany, France, etc. on Govt. order, '08-12; resumed duty at the Kyoto Imp. Univ.; attended the Carnegie Peace Foundation Conference held in '14 at Geneva; Prof. at Kyoto Imp. Univ. till '24. Works: Manuals of Political Economy; Lectures on Taxes; Lectures on Economics, etc. Add. Finance Dept., Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Ogawa, Heikichi** Barrister, M.P., ex-Min. of Railways; b. 1869 in Nagano-ken; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '92; Dir. Census Bureau on its creation in '20; left it in '22 when its status was lowered; Minister of Justice in the Coalition Cabinet, '25; Min. of Railways in the Tanaka Cabinet, '27-29. Add. Kojimachi Uchisaiwaicho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Oishi, Masami** a retired politician; b. 1855 in Kochi-ken; became follower of Count Itagaki, but left him '82; was arrested '85 about dynamite affairs, and confined some months; travelled abroad about '86; Min. Res. in Korea, '92-93; joined the Opposition led by Count Okuma; Vice-Minister of Agr. and Com. when the Matsukata Cabinet concluded an entente with the Progressives, '96-97, and next Minister of the same Dept., '98, in the Okuma-Itagaki coalition ministry; retired from political world in '15. Add. Nakano, near Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Oka, Kishichiro** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '14); b. 1866 in Okayama; grad. from Law, Tokyo Imp., Univ., '91 and successively held the post of secretary in the provinces, '93-04; Governor of Akita, '04-05; Chief Police Commissioner at Korean Residency-Gen. in '05; and deputy Dir. of Agr., Ind. & Com. Dept. and of the Interior in Korea, '07-10; Governor of Tottori, '10-13; Dir. of Police Bureau, Home Office, '13-14; Sup't of Metropolitan Police, '18-21. Add. Aoyama Minamicho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Okada, Ryohei** Crown Mem. House of Peers; b. 1864 in Shizuoka-ken; grad. from Literature, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '87; was teacher of the Tokyo High School till '93; Councillor of Dept. of Education and next Director of Yamaguchi High School; Vice-Minister of Education, '01-03; Deputy President of the Peers' School, '06-07; Pres. of Kyoto Imp. Univ., '07; Vice-Minister of Education, '08-11; Minister of same, '16-18 and again in '24-27. Add. Koishikawa Haramachi, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 656](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n656/mode/1up))

**Okazaki, Kunisuke** M.P., ex-Min. Agriculture & Forestry, Dir. of Keihan Electric Co., etc. Is more widely known as a politician who works behind the scene; was a confidant of the late Count Mutsu and was 1st Councillor of Communication Dept. when the late Hoshi held the portfolio, but has only short Govt. career, having been once a Chief Police Inspector; was adviser to the Furukawa Mining Co. Add. Shiba Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 657](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n657/mode/1up))

**Okuma, Nobutsune** Marquis (2nd), adopted son and heir of the late Shigenobu Okuma; b. 1870 in Tokyo, 3rd son of the late Count Matsu-ura; grad. from Peers School in '93 and then at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '97; at Cambridge, '05-07; Personal Secy. to Premier, '14-16; M.P., '15-16; Parl. Jun. Secy. of Edu. Dept. and then of Foreign Dept., '15. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 657](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n657/mode/1up))

**Oyama, Ikuo** former Prof. Waseda University which he graduated in Politics and Economy, class '05; b. 1880 in Hyogo-ken; sent by the Univ. to England for study and stayed there four years; on the editorial staff of the Osaka Asahi in '17 which he resigned in '18 and founded with his friends a monthly "We"; organized in '27 with others the Ronoto (Labor & Farmers Party) whih was later dissolved. Works: Democratic Culture, Social Foundation of Politics, Social Education, etc. Add. Toyamagahara, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 657](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n657/mode/1up))

**Ozaki, Yukio** M.P. of unbroken record, ex-Minister of Justice; b. 1859 in Miye-ken; studied at Keio, and for a short while held post of Jun. Secretary at Central Govt., but soon became a cause champion of the clan Govt. for introducing party politics; was ordered to leave Tokyo by the coup d'etat in '87 and went to England, returning home on the eve of the summoning of the 1st session of the Diet to sit in it ever since. He was in '97 Chief Counsellor of the Foreign Office in the Matsukata Progressive Cabinet; Minister of Education in '98; resigned the post and severed connection with Count Okuma's party; joined Prince Ito's new party, left it in '03 but to rejoin in '10; Mayor of Tokyo, '13-14; Min. of Justice, '14-16; joined the Kenseikai Party in '21 but left it soon after. Married Miss Theodora, d. of Baron Ozaki by English lady. Add. Shinagawa, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 657](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n657/mode/1up))

**Saionji, Kimmochi** Prince (Marquis '84; Prince '22), formerly courtier, ex-Premier, Mem. House of Peers; b. 1839 at Kyoto and adopted as heir by the house; took part when under 20 in the grave councils of the Restoration; at 19 he was Commander-in-Chief of an Imperial Army; Gov. of Niigata-ken for a few months in '68; studied in France, '69-80; and brought home highly democratic notions issuing at one time a democratic paper. Then commenced his official career, first as Vice-Senator, '81; accompanied Prince (then Mr.) Ito to Europe and U.S.A. to investigate Parliamentary system, '82; Senator, '83; Minister to Austria, '85; then to Berlin, '88; President of the Board of Decoration, '91; Vice-Pres. Code Investigation Commission and Vice-Pres. House of Peers, '93; Privy Councillor, '94; held portfolio of Education in the 2nd Ito Cabinet, '94-96; temporarily acting as Min. of Foreign Affairs; Min. of Education in the 3rd Ito Cabinet and President of the Privy Council, and as President of the Privy Council he had to occupy the post of Prime Minister ad interim no less than three times during the intervals between the resignation of one Ministry and the formation of another; became leader of the Seiyukai, '03 and twice formed his own Cabinet in '06-07 and '11-12; represented Japan as Peace Envoy at Versailles, '18 and was invested with the Grand Order of Merit. He is brother to the late Prince Tokudaiji and Baron Kichizaemon Sumitomo (died '26). Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 657](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n657/mode/1up))

**Sengoku, Mitsugu** D. Eng.; b. 1857 in Kochi; a pioneer grad. of Eng. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ. which he finished in '79; long connected with the Govt. R'lys and was Pres. of Kyushu R'ly Co. till its nationalization in '11; promoted a Hydro-Elec. Co., '11; toured in Europe, '11-12; Pres. of Inawashiro Hydro-Elec. Co., '12-14; Pres. of Imp. Govt. Rlys, '14-15; Min. of Railways, '24-26. Add. Azabu Fujimi-cho, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 657](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n657/mode/1up))

**Shidehara, Kijuro** Baron (cr. '20), Foreign Minister; b. 1872 in Nara-ken; grad. in Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '95 and served in Agr. & Com. Dept.; Eleve-Consul at Chemulpo, '99; transferred to London, Antwerp, etc.; Sec. at Head Office, '11; Embassy Counsellor at Washington in '12; Minister at The Hague, '14-15; Vice-Min. of Foreign Affairs in '15; Ambassador to U.S.A., '19-22; Min. of Foreign Affairs, '24-27 and again in July '29. Add. Hongo Kamifujimaye, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Shoda, Kazue** ex-Min. of Education, Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '14); b. 1869 in Ehime-ken; grad. in Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '95; passed the higher civil service examination the following year; inspector of taxation affairs, '07; Dir. of Finance Bureau, Treasury, '07; Vice-Min. of Finance, '11-14, having been in the meanwhile Chief of Hakodate Customs House, Sec. at the Head Office, etc.; Gov. of Chosen Bank in '15; again Vice-Minister of Finance and then Minister, '16-18; Minister of Education in the Tanaka Cabinet, '28-29. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Sugawara, Michitaka** Mem. House of Peers (since '16) and for several years Mng.-Dir. of the Japan-American Trust Co.; b. 1869 in Miyagi-ken; grad. in Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '96; entered the service of the Treasury; held post of Dir. of Revenue, Customs Offices at Hakodate, Kobe, etc.; Councillor in '04, then Dir. Tax Bureau; Vice-Min. of Finance, '15-16. Add. Koishikawa Kagocho, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Fujiya** Lawyer, M.P. for Tokyo, Chief Secretary of the Cabinet since July '29; b. 1882 in Oita-ken; grad. at Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ.; toured abroad '06-08; Parl. Counsellor, Home Office, '24-27. Add. Shibuya-machi, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Suzuki, Kisaburo** D.C.L., ex-Home Minister; b. 1867 in Kawasaki; grad. from Law Coll. of the Tokyo Imp. Univ. in '91, and sat on the bench since '95; meanwhile occupied the posts of Chief Judge at Tokyo District Court, Dir. Legal Affairs Bur., etc.; was dispatched abroad in '08 on a tour of inspection; Vice-Min. of Justice, '14; Procurator-Gen., '21-24; Min. of Justice, '24; Minister of Home Affairs, '28-29. Add. Koj. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Tagawa, Daikichiro** M.P., Pres. Meiji Gakuin; b. 1869 in Nagasaki-ken; grad. from the Waseda Semmon Gakko (now Univ.) in '90; took part in Sino-Japanese and Russo-Japanese Wars as interpreter; is a prominent Christian thinker; was imprisoned in '18 on charge of lese majeste for a magazine article; Parl. Secy. of Justice Dept., in Okuma Cabinet; Deputy-Mayor of Tokyo, '23-25. Add. Kobinatadaimachi, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Takahashi, Korekiyo** ex-M.P., ex-Visc.; b. 1845 in Tokyo; sent to America for study '67; returned home the following year; an official of the Dept. of Agr. and Com., '81, ultimately rising to be Dir. Patent Bureau; was involved in the Peruvian silver mine fraud concocted by a German imposter and resigned the post in '87; entered the Bank of Japan, and was made a Director; Vice-Pres. of the Yokohama Specie Bank, '97; elected Deputy-Gov. of Bank of Japan; elected Pres. of the Specie Bank, '06, while retaining the former post; was Financial agent in England and America, and raised loans for the Government in '05 and '06; Gov. of the Bank of Japan in '11; Minister of Finance, '13-14, and again in '18; promoted to Visc. in '20 for his service in connection with the European War; Premier, '21-22; resigned the Peerage in favor of his son and seat in the Upper House to contest the seat for Morioka in '24; resigned Leadership of Seiyukai, '25; Minister of Finance in the Tanaka Cabinet, Apr.-May, '27; became adviser to the Yasuda Holding Co., '29. Add. Akasaka Omote-cho, Tokyo. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 658](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n658/mode/1up))

**Taketomi, Tokitoshi** Mem. House of Peers (since '24); b. 1855 in Saga-ken; from obscure politician of local reputation he suddenly came to the front as soon as he began to sit in the Lower House where he was returned almost without a break from the first session; Chief Secretary of the Cabinet in Okuma-Itagaki Ministry; Minister of Communications in 2nd Okuma Ministry; occupied portfolio of Finance, '15-16; retired from active politics in '24 but still acts as Adviser to the Minsei Party. Add. Ushigome Kaga-cho, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Tanaka, Gi-ichi** Baron (cr. '20), Gen. (ret.), ex-Premier & Minister of Foreign Affairs; Leader of Seiyukai since '25; b. 1863 in Yamaguchi; Sub-Lieut., '86; grad. from the Staff Coll., '98; Dir. of Military Affairs Bureau, '10-13; despatched abroad, '13; Com. of the 2nd Infantry Brigade; Lieut.-Gen. and Vice-Chief of General Staff, '15; Minister of War in Hara, Takahashi and Yamamoto Cabinets; organized a ministry in Apr. '27. Add. Aoyama Kitamachi, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Tokonami, Takejiro** M.P., Leader of the former Seiyu-honto Party; b. 1863 in Kagoshima; grad. in politics, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90; entered civil service at the Home Office, either at head office or local office, in which he rose to be Governor of Tokushima-ken, '01; Dir. Local Administration, '08; Vice-Minister, '11-12; Pres. of Imp. Govt. Rlys., '13; Minister of Home Affairs, '18-22. Add. Azabu Mikawadai, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Tokugawa, Iyesato** Prince (cr. '84), Pres. of the House of Peers since '03; b. 1863 at Tokyo, 3rd son of Yoshiyori Tokugawa, one of the three Tokugawa branches, and was adopted as heir in '08 by the last of the Tokugawa Shoguns; studied in England, '73-77; toured abroad, '10; a Japanese Delegate at the Washington Disarmament Conference in '21. Add. Sendagaya, near Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Toyama, Mitsuru** Political boss, ex-Pres. of the Gen-yosha (a political club); b. 1855 in Fukuoka-ken; was thrown into prison for having sided with the rebels in the Civil War of '77; now stands aloof from politics; has befriended Chinese and other revolutionists. Add. Azabu Reinanzaka, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Uchigasaki, Sakusaburo** M.P., Parl. Counsellor of the Home Office (since July '29), Prof. at Waseda Univ.; b. 1877 in Miyagi-ken; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ. (English Literature) and is one of the pupils of Lafcadio Hearn; also studied at Oxford, '08-11; again went abroad, '20-21. Works: Life and Literature, Religion of Contemporaries, Lloyd George, Lincoln, etc. Add. Waseda Univ. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Uyehara, Etsujiro** Dr. Sc. (London Univ.), M.P.; b. 1878 in Nagano-ken; grad. from the Univ. of Washington, U.S.A.; lecturer at the Tokyo Higher Tech. Sch., '11-14; Prof. at Meiji Univ., '13-20; Parl. Counsellor of Dept. of Commerce & Industry in '24; Parl. Counsellor of the Foreign Office, '27-29; is Silver medalist of Univ. of London. Works: The Political Development of Japan (English), Studies on the Constitution of Japan (Japanese), Democratic Institution in Japan (Jap.), etc. Add. Aoyama Takagicho, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Uyeno, Yasutaro** M.P.; b. 1865 in Toyama-ken; was in the local government service for years; next a newspaper editor and businessman; Sen. Sec. to the Dept. of Justice in the Seiyukai Administration, '20. Add. Azabu Kogai-cho, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Jotaro** M.P., Pres. South Manchuria Rly. Co. ('27-29), ex-Executive Dir. of Mitsui Bussan; b. 1867 in Fukui; entered the service of the Mitsui Company in '82, and was attached to the Branch at Shanghai, where he soon rose to be its Manager and remained till '07; transferred to the head office in '12; was involved in the Navy Scandal. Add. Akasaka Shinsaka-machi, Tokyo. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 659](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n659/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Tatsuo** Baron (cr. '20), Mem. House of Peers, adviser to the Minsei Party, ex-Gov. of Japan Hypothec Bank; b. 1856 in Oita-ken; edu. at Keio; connected with educational work, '80-82; entered Mitsubishi Firm, '82; Dir. of Nippon Yusen Kaisha; entered in '90 Bank of Japan in which he was appointed Dir. of Banking Bureau and next Governor, 98-03; Gov. of Hypothec Bank of Japan, '10; Minister of Agr. & Com., '11-12 and again in '13-14 and for the third time in '18-22. Add. Kami-Nibancho, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto, Teijiro** D. Ph., ex-Min. of Agr. & Forestry, Chair. of board of directors of the Taiwan Sugar Mfg. Co., M.P.; b. 1870 in Niigata-ken; studied at the Halle and Leipzig Univ. in Germany for eight years; returned to Parliament seven times; Min. of Agr. & For. in the Tanaka Ministry, '27-29. Add. Kami-Meguro Gohongi, near Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Yoshiuye, Sho-ichiro** P.M., leader of the Seiyukai; b. 1865 in Chiba; was for a long time in Hokkaido as editor of a local paper and for which he first entered House of Representatives but now for his native province; visited Chile years ago on Govt. commission to investigate condition of immigrants; Parl. Vice-Min. of Com. & Ind., '27. Add. Akasaka Daimachi, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa, Kurahei** Mem. House of Peers (nom. '16); b. 1874 in Fukushima; grad. in Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '89; Secy. in Local Govts.; then Secy. and Dir. of Local Administration Bureau, Home Office; a prefectural Gov.; Dir. of Police Bureau, Home Office, '15-16. Add. Ushigome Akagishita, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Wakatsuki, Reijiro** ex-Prime Minister, Crown Mem. House of Peers, Adviser to the Minsei Party; b. 1866 in Shimane-ken; grad. in Law, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '92; Counsellor of the Treasury soon after graduation; Bureau Dir., '03; Vice-Min., '05; Financial Agent in London in '06; recalled home and appointed for a second time Vice-Minister of Finance, '68; resigned in '11; Minister of Finance, '12-12 and '14-15; Prime Minister as successor to Viscount Kato, also leader of the Kensei-kai party; resigned in Apr. '27. Add. Komagome Kamifujimaye, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

### Publicists

**Baba, Tsunego** Political Writer; b. 1875 in Okayama-ken; edu. at Doshisha and Waseda Univ.; was on the staff of the Japan Times for 10 years; then connected with the Oriental Information Agency in New York for four years; Mng.-Editor of the Kokumin Shimbun for several years. Works: "Cry for Reconstruction," "Labor Problems & General Election," etc. Add. Yotsuya Minami-Teramachi, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Ichikawa, Fusae** leader of woman's movement; b. 1893 in Aichi-ken; teacher for years, writer of Nagoya Shimbun, organized the new Women's Association with Miss Hiratsuka; visited America to inspect woman movement there in '21; entered Tokyo branch of International Labor Office in '23; joined the league of woman enfranchisement of which she is Director in '24; attended the Pan-Pacific women conference at Hawaii, '28; visited America to observe the condition of public education. Add. Sugamo-machi, near Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Ishimoto, Keikichi** 2nd Baron, Social reformer, eldest son of late Lieut.-Gen. Shinroku Ishimoto (War Minister in the 2nd Saionji Cabinet); b. 1887 in Tokyo; grad. from Mining, Tokyo Imp. Univ., '14 and was for years connected with Mitsui Mining Co. The Baroness also takes active part in social reform work, and the two are earnest advocates of birth control principle. Add. Takata-Toyokawacho, Tokyo. — p. 87 ([scan leaf 660](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n660/mode/1up))

**Nitobe, Inazo** Crown Mem. House of Peers (nom. '27), D. Agr., D.L., late of League of Nations Office (Geneva), ex-Prof. Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1861 in Iwate-ken; grad. Sapporo Agr. Coll., '81; studied political economy at Johns Hopkins' Univ. and at Bonn, Halle, Berlin; Prof. at the Sapporo College for several years; an official in the Formosan Govt., which he left in '04 to fill chair of Economics at the Kyoto Imp. Univ.; selected as first Japan-America exchange professor in '11. Works: "Bushido," published at Philadelphia in '98; "Nogyo Honron" (Principle of Agriculture) and others. Add. Kobinatadaimachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

**Oku, Umeo** leader of woman's movement; b. 1895 in Fukuoka-ken; grad. at domestic economics, Nippon Women's Univ. in '16; on grad. joined the women movement and now leads the Society of Woman Salary Earners and edits its organ, "Woman Movement." Add. Ushigome Ichigaya-Hommura-cho, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

**Sakatani, Yoshiro** Baron (cr. '07), D.L., ex-Min. of Finance and ex-Mayor of Tokyo; b. 1863 at Okayama, son of a celebrated scholar of Chinese classics; grad. from the Tokyo Imp. Univ., and entered the Finance Dept., '87, with which he was long connected without a break, having been successively Counsellor, Accountant, Dir. of Accountant Bureau, Vice-Min. in 01, and Minister in '06-08; toured abroad, '00; Vice-President of Census Com., '10; attended the Peace Conference at The Hague, '11; Mayor of Tokyo, '12-15; despatched to Paris in '16 to represent Japan at the Financial Conference of the Allied Powers. Add. Koishikawa Hara-machi, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

**Sawada, Ken** a political writer; b. 1894 in Tottori-ken; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ., in Law, class '18; commissioner of the Foreign Dept.; member of the Tokyo Municipal Research Commission. Works: Revolution of International Politics, Outline of the League of Nations. Add. Sendagaya, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

**Tsurumi, Yusuke** Writer and M.P.; b. 1885 in Gumma-ken; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ. in Law; was long on civil service, the last as Secretary of the Govt. Railway Dept. which he resigned in '22; was resident officer for the Railways during '16-19 in America and Europe; lectured at Brown Institute on Japan-American relations in '25; toured through U.S.A., '27. Works: South Sea Travel, Impressions of Great Men, in Europe and America, Americanism and Future of the Japan-American Relations, Stories of Three Cities, Contemporary Japan (in English), etc. Add. Azabu Sangenya, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

### Religion

**Ebina, Danjo** one of the veteran pastors, and formerly Pres. of the Doshisha, Kyoto; b. 1856 in Yanagawa; studied at the Foreign Language School in Kumamoto; then theology at the Doshisha; has subsequently led an active life in evangelistic and educational work; pastor of the Hongo Church, Tokyo, for a long time; Pres. of the Doshisha, Kyoto, '20-29. Add. Kyoto. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

**Ibuka, Kajinosuke** M.A. (Princeton), D.D. (Rutgers), Hon. Pres. of the Meiji Gakuin, Chairman of National Committee, Y.M.C.A. Union of Japan, Vice-Chairman of World Students Fed.; b. in Aizu; studied at Dr. Brown's School in Yokohama and then at the Union Theological Seminary, New York; represented Japan in the World's Y.M.C.A. and other religious conferences held abroad. Mrs. Ibuka is B.A. (Mount Holyoke, Mass.) Add. Meiji Gakuin, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 88 ([scan leaf 661](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n661/mode/1up))

**Kosaki, Kodo** famous pioneer Christian and pastor of Reinanzaka Church (Tokyo), Chairman of the Kumi-ai Churches of Japan, of Sunday School Society of Japan, and of Christian Church Union of Japan; b. 1856 in Kumamoto-ken; studied at Kumamoto English School and Doshisha, Kyoto; Pres. of Doshisha Univ. in '90-97; three times represented Japan at International Congress of Religions abroad. Add. Reinanzaka, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Otani, Kozui** Count, ex-Lord Abbot of the Western Hongwanji Temple, one of the two greatest Buddhist centres in Japan; b. 1876 at Kyoto; studied long in Europe, and started for home, '02 via Central Asia, but was obliged to hurry home before he could carry out his program on learning of the death of his father; visited Saghalien and China, '07; and also India, '10; retiring from active service, '14, again visited Buddha land via Korea, China and Burmah, and is now staying in China. Add. Shanghai. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Tsunashima, Kakichi** Pastor of Bancho Congregational Church, Tokyo; b. 1860 in Okayama-ken; studied at Doshisha, and Yale Univ., U.S.A.; Pastor of the present church for about twenty years; was Peace Envoy to U.S.A. in '13 representing Christian churches of Japan, and to Constance in '14 representing Japan. Add. Koj. Nakarokubancho, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Uchimura, Kanzo** Christian teacher and essayist; b. 1861 in Saitama; grad. from Sapporo Agr. Coll., '81; clerk at the Dept. of Agr. and Commerce; studied at Amherst Univ., '83-88; instructor at the 1st High School, Tokyo, and from religious scruples he had to resign; then took to journalism; and is now educationalist and non-Sectarian Christian teacher; is publishing a religious monthly "Seisho-no-Kenkyu"; started in '26 another religious magazine Christian Intelligencer which was later discontinued. Wrote "Representative Men of Japan," "How I Became a Christian," etc. Add. Tsunohazu, near Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Yamamuro, Gumpei** Lieut.-Commissioner of the Salvation Army in Japan; b. 1871 in Okayama-ken; raised himself from a printer's apprentice and studied at Doshisha; entered Salvation Army in '95; travelled through Europe, '25-26; is known as an influential preacher as well as an able social reformer; has written a number of Christian works. Add. Salvation Army Headquarters, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

### Science

**Aso, Keijiro** D. Agr., Prof. of Agr. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1875 in Tokyo; grad. from Agr. Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ., with honors in '99; then studied at the University Hall; Assist.-Prof. at his alma mater, '01; sent to Germany, etc. for study of agricultural chemistry, '08-12. Add. Yotsuya Minami-Teramachi, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Fujii, Kenjiro** D. Sc., Emer. Prof. Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1866 in Ishikawa-ken; grad. in Science, Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '92; went to Germany for study, '01; appointed Prof. at his alma mater, May '12. Add. Hongo-ku, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Fujisawa, Rikitaro** Dr. Sc., Mathematician, Emeritus Prof. at Tokyo Imp. Univ., Mem. House of Peers (elected '24); b. 1861 in Niigata-ken; pursued his special study in England and Germany, '83-87; Exchange Lecturer in U.S.A., '22; has written several works on mathematics. Add. Koishikawa Suwacho, Tokyo. — p. 89 ([scan leaf 662](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n662/mode/1up))

**Fujita, Keishin** Prof. of Fishery at Hokkaido Imp. Univ.; b. 1879 in Tokyo; grad. from Sapporo Agr. Coll., '90 and Tokyo Imp. Univ., '92; further studied in England and Germany. Add. Sapporo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 663](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n663/mode/1up))

**Fujiwara, Sakuhei** D. Sc., Meteorologist; b. 1884 in Nagano-ken; grad. at the Tokyo Imp. Univ. in Theoretical Physics, '09; appointed assistant at the Central Meteorological Observatory the same year; granted the Doctorate, '15; promoted to Meteorologist (titular) in '18; proceeded to Europe on official business, '20; Prof. at his alma mater as additional duty, '2[illegible]; attended the International Meteorological Observatory directors conference, '26. Is specialized in Theoretical Meteorology, especially vortex motions. Add. Central Meteo. Observatory, Tokyo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 663](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n663/mode/1up))

**Goto, Seitaro** D.Sc., Zoologist, Mem. Imp. Academy, Prof. & Dean of Science Coll., Tokyo Imp. Univ.; b. 1867 in Yamaguchi-ken; edu. at Tokyo Imp. Univ., '90, and further at the Univ. Hall, and went to America to study entomology; attended 3rd International Zoological Conference held at Leyden, Holland, '95; Prof. at First High School on return and next at the present institution. Add. Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 663](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n663/mode/1up))

**Hatai, Shinkishi** Ph. D., Biologist, Prof. at Faculty of Science, Tohoku Imp. Univ. (Sendai), Prof. at Pennsylvania Univ. (U.S.A.); educated at Tohoku Gakuin; studied zoology under Prof. Goto at 1st High School (Tokyo); studied at Chicago University and appointed lecturer of the alma mater upon graduation; later Prof. at Cincinnati University and then at Pennsylvania University and Dir. of Biological Dept. of the Wistar Institute attached to the Univ.; appointed Prof. at Tohoku Imp. Univ., '23 while holding professorship at Pennsylvania Univ. Add. Wistar Institute, Pennsylvania Univ. (U.S.A.) & Tohoku Imp. Univ. (Sendai). — p. 90 ([scan leaf 663](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n663/mode/1up))

**Hatta, Saburo** D.Sc., Prof. of Zoology & Entomology, Hokkaido Imp. Univ.; b. 1865 in Kumamoto. Grad. from the Coll. Sc., Tokyo Imp. Univ., class '91; Prof. at the Peers School, '93; next at the Sapporo Agr. Coll.; visited America, '06; sent for study to Europe, '12-15. Add. Sapporo. — p. 90 ([scan leaf 663](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1930/page/n663/mode/1up))

**Hayashi, Tsuruichi** D.Sc., Mathematician, ex-Prof. at Tohoku Imp. Univ. (Sendai); b. 1873 in Tokushima-ken; edu. at Coll. of Science, Tokyo Imp. Univ.; Assist.-Prof. Kyoto Imp. Univ., '98; Prof. Tokyo Higher Normal School, '07; Prof. Tohoku Imp. Univ., '11; Dean of Sc. Coll. of same till '29 when he retired from the post; is associate of various scientific societies of Germany, France, Italy, etc. Add.
